40. Operation in Tropical Climates                     located as dustproof as possible with available

   When operated in tropical climates, radio equip-    materials.
ment may be installed in tents, huts, or, when
necessary, in underground dugouts. When equip-         c. Never tie power cords, signal cords, or other
ment is installed below ground and when it is set
up 1n swampy areas, moisture conditions are more       wire connections to the outside or inside of a tent.
acute than normal. Ventilation is usually very         Desert areas are subject to sudden wind squalls
poor, and the high relative humidity causes con-
densation of moisture on the equipment whenever        which may jerk the connections loose or break the

                                        CHAPTER 6

                                 FIELD MAINTENANCE

       Note. This chapter contains information pertinent to field maintenance. The amount of repair that can be per-
formed by units having field maintenance responsibility is limited only by the tools and test equipment available and by
the skill of the repairman.

                   TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

Section I. SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE

126. General                                           c. Check the radio set control to be sure that no
                                                    wires are attached to the terminal board. Tighten

repack the equipment in the original manner.        hold it to the rack on one side and by loosening

followed for disassembly.                           two screws in the rack and tighten them to prevent
                                                    loss.

                                                       d. Remove the transmitters and receivers in the
   To disassemble the equipment for repacking,      same manner.
follow the procedure outlined in paragraphs 16
through 22. The following is a step-by-step pro-       e. Remove the antenna jack support from the
cedure for disassembly.                             rack member, and replace the mounting screws
                                                    in the rack.
   a. Remove all external connections, such as
antenna, ground, ac power, and interconnections       f. Remove the rack control panel and inter-
between the rack and the remote console.            connect the cable from the rack by unsnapping

   b. To work in the back of the main unit, remove  the panel to the rack. Replace the mounting

Section Il. DEMOLITION OF MATERIEL TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

128. General                                           b. Out. Cut cords and cables; use axes, hand-
                                                    axes, or machetes.
   The demolition procedure outlined in paragraph
129 will be used to prevent the enemy from using       c. Burn. Burn transmitters, receivers, control
or salvaging this equipment. Demolition of the      units, consoles, connecting cables and technical
equipment will be accomplished only upon order      manuals; use gasoline, kerosene, oil, flamethrowers,
of the commander.                                   or incendiary grenades.

129. Methods of Destruction                            d. Bend. Bend panels and chassis; use sledge
                                                    hammers and crowbars.
   a. Smash. Smash tubes, coils, and transform-
ers; use sledges, axes, pickaxes, hammers, crow-       e. Explode. If explosives are necessary, use
bars or heavy tools.                                grenades, or TNT.
